# Backlog

The queue of work this repo owes: **what is owed, by whom, and measured how.**

Read this **before starting anything** (step 0 of the dev loop). An item that lives only in a scratchpad, a
subagent report, or a chunk's own criteria is a deletion with extra steps — nothing reads those at the start of
the next piece of work.

## Rules for this file

1. **Every quantity names its command or `file:line`.** A number without an instrument rots silently, and this
   repo has already shipped counts that were wrong in three documents at once.
2. **Record at disposal, reconcile at the push.** When a check or a round disposes of a finding as "later",
   append it here *then* — not at the end. Residuals are created many waves before a push.
3. **No diary.** This is not a log of what was done. Closed items are deleted, not archived. Use `git log` for
   history.
4. **Owner-only items are never started by an agent.** They are listed so they are not forgotten, not so they
   are picked up.
5. **The loop does not stop while this file has entries.** Finishing a chunk is not finishing the work — the
   next action is always the next backlog item. See "How the loop consumes this file" below.
6. **All three sections are hand-ordered by the owner, and it is PRIORITY order — top to bottom in the source
   file, in Owner decisions and Verification owed exactly as in Next.** There is no `priority:` field; order
   IS the field. No section is sorted by id or by when an item was added, and a tool that resorts one destroys
   information the owner put there on purpose (backlog N31, ruling R-BL-1).

## How the loop consumes this file

**While this file is non-empty, the dev loop does not stop.** At the end of every chunk, wave, or ruling, the
next action is read off this file rather than chosen freshly.

| Section | What the loop does |
|---|---|
| **Next — actionable now** | Work it. Top to bottom unless a dependency says otherwise. This is where the loop spends its time. |
| **Owner decisions outstanding** | **Never started.** When these are all that remain, the loop's action is to **put them to the owner as a decision** — once, with what is blocked and what would unblock it. Escalating is a step; idling is not. |
| **Verification owed** | Same: surface it, with the exact instrument the owner would run. |

**The loop ends a turn only after it has either advanced an actionable item or escalated a blocked one.**
Stopping with actionable entries present and neither done is the failure this rule exists to prevent.

### Disjoint items are worked SIMULTANEOUSLY

The loop does not work the backlog one item at a time when items do not touch each other. Before dispatching,
compute each candidate item's **file set** and run them together when the sets do not intersect.

**Disjointness has two halves, and both must hold. File-disjoint alone is not enough** — that lesson cost this
repo a whole contract mechanism when two design seats with non-overlapping outputs produced two incompatible
schemas for the same table, because each was designing against facts the other was still establishing.

1. **Exact-path disjointness — computed, never eyeballed.** An item's set is *the files it edits* **plus the
   tests that assert on the behaviour it changes**. Intersect the sets mechanically (`sort | uniq -d`, empty
   output) and paste the result.
2. **Informational independence.** Ask of each pair: *does either establish a fact the other designs against?*
   If yes they are coupled however disjoint their files are — sequence them, or extract the shared contract
   into its own earlier step, alone.

**Cap a simultaneous wave at 2–3 items.** A larger fan-out has produced duplicated discovery here — three
agents independently finding the same blocker, and one designing a solution to a problem a sibling was
concurrently proving did not exist.

**Worked example, run 2026-09-13 — and it refuted the obvious answer.** `SEC-1` (item 1) looked like the ideal
parallel candidate: a one-file resolver fix, unrelated to interview-prep. Computed:
`grep -rln "featureEngine\|wantsEmbedded" --include=*.js app lib` → **58 files** (canary
`zzNoSuchSymbolzz` → 0), and the set **includes `app/api/interview-prep/route.test.js` and
`lib/copilot/groundingNotice.js`** — files IP3 and IP-N own. **Not disjoint.** SEC-1 waits.
Items 2 and 3 (`scripts/regression/**` and a regression AC ruling) *are* disjoint from IP3's
`lib/interviewPrep/**`, and run alongside it.

**A blocked item is escalated once, not repeatedly.** Re-asking the same unanswerable question every turn is
spinning wearing a decision's clothes. Once surfaced, it stays listed and silent until the owner answers or the
blocker clears.

The orchestrator's ruling that follows from it, also settled: THE SAFETY LINE MOVES FROM INSERTION TO EGRESS Ã¢â‚¬â€ inserting an unreviewed fact into a draft is harmless; an unreviewed fact reaching an employer is not. Because `app/components/preview/CopyDocumentControl.js:80` sets `onMouseDown={(e) => e.preventDefault()}` deliberately so a pointer click never blurs the neighbouring field (legitimate Ã¢â‚¬â€ see :74-79, do not remove), a mouse/touch user who types a new name and clicks "Copy file name" WITHOUT tabbing away copies the PREVIOUS name. Keyboard users are unaffected: Tab/Enter blurs and commits first. **SAME CLASS, NOT A SECOND BUG Ã¢â‚¬â€ Download is stale too.** `handleDownload` (:419) never calls `commitFileName`, and the download filename resolves downstream from the committed value (`app/hooks/useDocumentPreview.js:481-490` renameDocument, :503-536 downloadDocumentPreview reading `tailoringMapRef.current` which updates one render late at :93-95, and `lib/tailor/documentScopes.js:38-57` buildDownloadArgs reading `entry.resumeFileName`/`entry.coverLetterFileName`) Ã¢â‚¬â€ so even a same-tick `commitFileName()` cannot fix Download; the draft must be conveyed directly. THE CLASS: every action that CONSUMES the file name must see what the user is looking at. **THE CORRECT PATTERN ALREADY EXISTS IN THIS FILE:** `handleSaveToDrive` (:489-498) calls `commitFileName()` AND passes `fileNameDraft.trim()` DIRECTLY to `drive.saveToDrive` (comment :484-488 explains it is avoiding this exact one-tick staleness) Ã¢â‚¬â€ Drive save is therefore already correct and must stay so. The fix follows it: Copy reads the live draft (re-resolved through `resolveDocumentFileName` so copy==download survives sanitising), and Download conveys `fileNameDraft.trim()` to its consumer rather than relying on committed parent state. **CROSS-FILE JOIN:** the Download half is not complete in the dialog alone Ã¢â‚¬â€ the consumer (`useDocumentPreview.downloadDocumentPreview` / `buildDownloadArgs`) must prefer the conveyed name over the stored `entry.*FileName`, or the fix ships inert. Next: an implementer applies the fix following `handleSaveToDrive` Ã¢â‚¬â€ (1) `DocumentPreviewDialog.js`: compute the copied/rendered title from `fileNameDraft` (re-resolved), and have `handleDownload` convey `fileNameDraft.trim()` to its consumer; (2) the download consumer (`app/hooks/useDocumentPreview.js` downloadDocumentPreview / `lib/tailor/documentScopes.js` buildDownloadArgs) must prefer the conveyed name over the stored committed name so the join is not inert. | N69 | **OWNER REQUEST 2026-09-27 (ownerÃ¢â‚¬â„¢s own words): "i need the ability to see where something is going to spill over into multiple pages for the review/edit modal, and i need the ability to adjust line spacing and paragraph spacing".** **SEQUENCED SECOND IN THIS SECTION, DIRECTLY BEHIND N61, BY OWNER RULING 2026-09-27** Ã¢â‚¬â€ the owner said facts work "needs to be top priority" once this item was already drafted as the lead item; a future session should not reorder this ahead of N61 without a fresh owner instruction. **TWO CAPABILITIES, AND THEY SHOULD LIKELY SPLIT.** (a) SEEING where content crosses a page boundary is a measurement problem; (b) CONTROLLING line/paragraph spacing is an editing problem that only becomes useful once (a) exists to show whether a spacing change actually fixed an overflow. **BUILD (a) FIRST**: it needs no new persisted state (the data it would measure Ã¢â‚¬â€ spaceBeforePt/spaceAfterPt Ã¢â‚¬â€ is already parsed into the render model, see below) and it is non-destructive, where (b) writes into a rebuild seam this repo already has an open, eight-round-unresolved defect on (backlog N54, below). **THE APP DOES NOT KNOW A PAGE HEIGHT TODAY, in any sense usable for an on-screen "this spills onto page two" indicator.** The live preview surface (`app/components/DocumentPreviewDialog.js`'s `pageSx`, `:66-88`) is an unbounded scrolling box Ã¢â‚¬â€ `minHeight: 360`, no `maxHeight`, no page-break rendering, no overflow measurement of any kind. A Letter page size (8.5in x 11in, `w:pgSz w:w="12240" w:h="15840"`) plus 1in margins (`w:pgMar w:top="1440" ...`) IS hardcoded in exactly two places, but neither is wired to the preview: `lib/document/docx.js:462-467` (`buildMinimalistDocumentXml`, used when assembling a fresh minimalist rÃƒÂ©sumÃƒÂ© docx) and `lib/document/combineDocuments.js:87-92` (`combinedDocumentXml`, the "Combine both documents" feature) both write this as OUTPUT section metadata for a docx being generated from scratch Ã¢â‚¬â€ neither reads it back to compute where rendered content would cross a page boundary. The one place real pagination happens anywhere in this codebase is `combineDocuments.js:127-139`'s `combinedHtml`, which sets `@page { size: Letter; margin: 1in; }` in a print stylesheet handed to `printCombinedHtml` (`:144` on) via a hidden iframe and the BROWSER'S OWN print engine Ã¢â‚¬â€ genuine page layout, but only for the explicit combine-and-print-to-PDF flow, never for the ordinary single-document preview, and never exposed as a live on-screen signal. **So "where it spills onto page two" has nothing to compute from today** Ã¢â‚¬â€ it would need a wholly new measurement, and this repo's own two existing pagination mechanisms (the hardcoded OOXML section size vs. the browser's `@page` print CSS) already coexist without one being derived from the other, which is itself evidence that a browser-side estimate and Word's real pagination are not guaranteed to agree. **PARAGRAPH SPACING IS ALREADY REPRESENTED; LINE SPACING IS NOT.** `lib/document/docxPreview.js` parses each paragraph's `<w:pPr><w:spacing w:before=".." w:after=".."/></w:pPr>` (twentieths of a point, OOXML's own unit) into `spaceBeforePt`/`spaceAfterPt` on the render model (`:159-161` the regex read, `:174-175` the model fields), and renders it straight back out as inline CSS margin (`renderParagraphHtml`, `:241`: `margin:${spaceBeforePt}pt 0 ${spaceAfterPt}pt`) Ã¢â‚¬â€ `lib/document/combineDocuments.js:64-66`'s `paragraphXml` writes the identical value back into a rebuilt docx, so the value round-trips today. Fidelity here is already pinned by a landed test: `lib/document/docxPreview.list.test.js:522-527` ("a header paragraph immediately after a list still carries its OWN spacing") exercises exactly this `w:spacing` behaviour, and `docxPreview.test.js`'s broader AC-C17 corpus pins the same parse-model-render chain a spacing feature must not break. LINE spacing (the space BETWEEN wrapped lines within one paragraph Ã¢â‚¬â€ OOXML's `w:spacing w:line`/`w:lineRule`) has zero references anywhere in `lib/document/` (grep-confirmed) Ã¢â‚¬â€ the preview instead hardcodes one uniform value for the whole document, `lineHeight: 1.3` in `DocumentPreviewDialog.js`'s `pageSx` (`:73`), independently duplicated as `line-height: 1.3` in `combineDocuments.js:135`'s print stylesheet. **No UI control anywhere lets a user change either value** Ã¢â‚¬â€ grep across `app/components`+`app/hooks` for a spacing-labelled control returns nothing; the model reads paragraph spacing only to stay faithful to the source template, never for editing. **THE REBUILD-SURVIVAL QUESTION IS THE SAME ONE BACKLOG N54 ALREADY HAS OPEN, UNRESOLVED AFTER EIGHT VERIFICATION ROUNDS, FOR A SIBLING PARAGRAPH PROPERTY.** Per the standing download-rebuild rule, an edited document's download rebuilds from the ENGINE docx rather than the uploaded template (`app/hooks/useDocumentPreview.js:492-548`'s `downloadDocumentPreview`, threading `templateDocxB64`/`templateDocxPath`; `lib/document/docx.js:536`'s single-source-of-truth rebuild function) Ã¢â‚¬â€ any spacing control's edits would have to survive that exact rebuild path, the same seam N54 documents as silently dropping a DIFFERENT paragraph-level property (a soft line break) across eight NOT-SHIP rounds because the pristine-reference callers were trusted to pass it, rather than it being structurally required. A spacing control should not be designed in ignorance of N54's still-open state Ã¢â‚¬â€ it lands on the identical seam. **ALSO OVERLAPS N59'S DURABILITY GAP.** Once a cover letter's accepted facts or a version switch blanks `coverLetterDocxB64` (`useDocumentPreview.js:255`), the download falls back to the generic uploaded template (per N59); a spacing edit made while that blanking is in effect would apply to the on-screen render model but has nothing to persist into, and would not survive a reload the same way N59 already documents for accepted-fact text. **N63 (copyable per-document titles) touches the same dialog file but not the same code** Ã¢â‚¬â€ sequence-compatible, no design dependency. **THE CENTRAL RISK, stated as plainly as N54's own eight rounds insist on**: a page-break indicator computed by measuring rendered HTML in a browser is an ESTIMATE, not Word's real pagination Ã¢â‚¬â€ different font-metric rounding, line-wrapping, and OOXML page-layout rules (widow/orphan control, keep-with-next, etc.) mean a browser-side "still fits on one page" can simply be wrong. A wrong indicator is worse than none: the candidate would trust it and send a two-page letter believing it was one. **AN OWNER DECISION IS NEEDED BEFORE DESIGN STARTS**: (1) is an ESTIMATED, browser-computed page-break indicator acceptable to ship at all, or must it wait for a real-page-geometry computation (e.g. a server-side docx layout pass) before the candidate is shown anything labelled a page boundary; and (2) should the spacing-control half be sequenced after N54's rebuild-seam fix lands, given it writes into the same fragile mechanism, or built in parallel with an explicit plan to re-verify against N54's eventual fix. | AN OWNER DECISION FIRST (estimated vs. real-geometry page-break indicator; whether spacing controls wait on N54's rebuild-seam fix), then a design round that scopes (a) the measurement/indicator ahead of (b) the spacing controls, per this item's own analysis, and settles whether a spacing setting is per-document or per-paragraph before any UI is built | app/components/DocumentPreviewDialog.js:66-88 (pageSx, the unbounded preview surface with no page-height/overflow concept) and :73 (lineHeight: 1.3, the one hardcoded uniform line-height); lib/document/docx.js:462-467 (buildMinimalistDocumentXml's hardcoded Letter w:pgSz/w:pgMar) and lib/document/combineDocuments.js:87-92 (combinedDocumentXml, the same constants) Ã¢â‚¬â€ both write page-size OOXML metadata for freshly-assembled output, neither reads it back to measure overflow; lib/document/combineDocuments.js:127-139 (combinedHtml's @page print stylesheet) and :144 (printCombinedHtml) Ã¢â‚¬â€ the one real pagination path in this codebase, browser-print-only, never wired to the live preview; lib/document/docxPreview.js:159-161 (w:spacing before/after parsed) and :174-175 (spaceBeforePt/spaceAfterPt on the model), :241 (rendered back as inline CSS margin); lib/document/combineDocuments.js:64-66 (paragraphXml writes the same value back into a rebuilt docx) Ã¢â‚¬â€ paragraph spacing already round-trips; lib/document/docxPreview.list.test.js:522-527 Ã¢â‚¬â€ the landed test pinning w:spacing fidelity a spacing feature must not break; no equivalent test or code path exists for w:line/w:lineRule (grep-confirmed zero references in lib/document/); app/hooks/useDocumentPreview.js:492-548 (downloadDocumentPreview, the templateDocxB64/templateDocxPath rebuild threading) and lib/document/docx.js:536 (the single-source-of-truth rebuild function) Ã¢â‚¬â€ the download-rebuild rule any spacing edit would have to survive; backlog N54 Ã¢â‚¬â€ eight NOT-SHIP verification rounds on the identical paragraph-property rebuild seam (a soft line break), unresolved; a spacing control lands on the same seam; backlog N59 Ã¢â‚¬â€ coverLetterDocxB64 is blanked on a cover-letter version switch (useDocumentPreview.js:255) and the download degrades to the uploaded template; a spacing edit made in that state inherits the identical non-durability; backlog N63 Ã¢â‚¬â€ shares DocumentPreviewDialog.js but not the same code; sequence-compatible, no design dependency |
| N96 | **OWNER REQUEST 2026-09-29, verbatim: "i also need actual indicators as to line and para spacing changing".** A FEEDBACK/INDICATOR layer over the ALREADY-SHIPPED N69 whole-document spacing control (app/components/SpacingControl.js, mounted at app/components/DocumentPreviewDialog.js:748 for both DOCX scope tabs). N69 applies spacing = {lineSpacing, paragraphSpacingPt}|null to BOTH documents at preview/download time; this chunk does NOT change how spacing is applied (AC-7 guards N69) -- it makes the current value, the applied/changed state, and default-vs-custom VISIBLE and ANNOUNCED. GROUNDING (verified read-only 2026-09-29): today the ONLY indicator is the active preset's variant=contained highlight (SpacingControl.js:52,68) -- there is NO textual readout ('1.5x line, 12pt paragraph'), NO default-vs-custom marker, NO way back to the null (source-default) state, and NO aria-pressed/aria-current on the active preset (MUI Button contained/outlined is purely visual, so a screen reader cannot tell which preset is active -- the N84 class). When spacing===null NO button is contained (all outlined), visually indistinguishable from 'nothing picked yet'. DOMAIN TRAP the indicator gets wrong by default (governs AC-1/AC-3/AC-4): null means 'use the document's OWN spacing' (source fidelity, N69 CB-R-1) -- it is NOT {lineSpacing:1, paragraphSpacingPt:0}. A readout that renders null as '1x/0pt', or a reset that writes {1,0} instead of null, is a LIE that destroys source fidelity while claiming to restore/report it; the safe direction is under-claiming ('Default / your document's own spacing'). WHOLE-DOCUMENT AND BOTH DOCS: setDocumentSpacing(scope,value) writes one entry.spacing per job and explicitly ignores scope (app/hooks/useDocumentPreview.js:495-504), shared across resume+cover, persisted to localStorage across reload (app/page.js:516-519); the indicator must reflect whole-document (not per-paragraph) scope and one setting governing both files, never imply granularity the app lacks. CORRECTION TO THE ORIGINAL BRIEF'S PREMISE: applySpacingToHtml (lib/document/docxPreview.js:355-373) is a PURE SYNCHRONOUS string transform -- the preview re-render on a spacing change is instantaneous, NOT async, so a pending/working spinner at preview time would be fabricated latency and is OUT of scope (resolveDocumentBlob runs on DOWNLOAD, which already has downloadStatus). MEASUREMENT HONESTY: jsdom does no layout, so the actual VISUAL spacing change (lines look more spaced) is OWNER-JUDGED in a real browser (AC-8); the automated criteria assert declared values + announced state only. ONE OWNER DECISION surfaced (AC-4): a reset-to-source-default control -- AC recommends shipping it (indicating an override without an exit is the N82 one-way-door defect), but if the owner wants indicators only this round, AC-4 defers the reset to its own item. AC written this round (docs/loop/N96.ac.r1.md); design/plan/tests to follow. PRIORITY/ORDER is the owner's to set -- filed adjacent to its parent feature N69; move these lines to reprioritize. | AC (docs/loop/N96.ac.r1.md) written this round + its fresh adversarial check; ONE OWNER DECISION (reset-to-default control in-scope now vs deferred [AC recommends ship it]); THEN 1c UX (the indicator surface: readout, default/custom marker, whole-document label, reset affordance, aria/live-region -- the small SpacingControl gains up to 5 elements; 6b cumulative-surface cost for the 8b re-judge) + 1e craft, reconciled with 1b structure (where the readout/default-custom/reset state lives; reuse the dialog's existing [role=status] live region at DocumentPreviewDialog.js:883-886 without breaking its lookup invariant; AC-2's widget choice -- ToggleButtonGroup sets aria-pressed natively, per mui-a11y traps); then plan, 4b RED tests, implementation, verify. | N74 | **A measurement limit worth recording before any auto-insertion work ships a concurrency claim: no test in this repo's current setup can prove a true mid-flight edit race between a candidate typing and an auto-insert write.** This repo's tests run under jsdom with React's `act()` (`hello-world/vitest.config.js`'s `test:` block sets `environment: "jsdom"`), and every sequenced-edit test in this area serializes through `await act(async () => { ... })` calls, each one fully committed before the next begins Ã¢â‚¬â€ for example the back-to-back `await act` blocks in `app/hooks/acceptFactIdSeam.test.js` (`:149,152,155,224,228`) and in the new coalesced-removal test `app/hooks/removeInsertedFact.coalesced.rc.test.js` (`:172,182,189,198,202,222,226`). | N78 | **THE INTERVAL HALF IS FIXED Ã¢â‚¬â€ `app/page.js:937` is now `30 * 60 * 1000`, landed 2026-09-28 in f9adecf, so the 30x gap below is HISTORY and any downstream citation of `60 * 1000` is stale (N79's "reduces N78 waste" claim shrinks by ~30x accordingly). WHAT REMAINS OPEN is the re-fire: the effect still calls `loadGmailMessages()` unconditionally before starting a fresh interval on every change to the application count, and the stale click-handler comment is still there. Original finding, kept for its evidence:** **THE POLL INTERVAL CONTRADICTED ITS OWN COMMENT BY 30x.** `app/page.js`'s background Gmail refresh effect is documented at `:920-922` as fetching "once after the user's applications load, then every 30 minutes while the tab is open," but the code that runs it, `:926`, is `setInterval(loadGmailMessages, 60 * 1000)` Ã¢â‚¬â€ 60 THOUSAND milliseconds, i.e. every 60 SECONDS, not the 1,800,000ms (30 minutes) the comment promises Ã¢â‚¬â€ a 30x gap between stated and actual cadence, read directly on the current tree, unmoved from the citation handed to me. So a signed-in user with at least one tracked application (`:924`'s guard, `if (!currentUser || applicationData.length === 0) return;`) leaving a tab open for an hour fires roughly 60 POSTs to `/api/gmail/messages` rather than the two the comment implies. **`/api/gmail/messages/route.js` imports no rate limiter** Ã¢â‚¬â€ confirmed by reading its full import list (`next/server`, `@/lib/supabase/server`, `@/lib/gmail/gmailClient`, `@/lib/supabase/upsertGmailMessages`) Ã¢â‚¬â€ so none of those refused calls is throttled; each still costs a serverless invocation, a Supabase `auth.getUser()` round trip, and a Redis cache read (`lib/gmail/gmailClient.js`'s `loadTokens`) before the route returns its 403. **THE EFFECT ALSO RE-FIRES, AND RESETS THE INTERVAL, ON EVERY CHANGE TO THE APPLICATION COUNT** Ã¢â‚¬â€ its dependency array (`:929`) is `[currentUser, applicationData.length]`, and the effect body calls `loadGmailMessages()` unconditionally (`:925`) before starting a fresh `setInterval` (`:926`) on every run. Applications load asynchronously and their count can change more than once in a session, and each such change fires an extra immediate call on top of whatever the running interval was already about to produce Ã¢â‚¬â€ the shape that would generate two close-together calls rather than a clean 60-second tick, consistent with the owner's downloaded activity log showing two 403s 45 seconds apart. **A second stale artifact in the same block**: the comment at `:901-902` reads "Used both by the icon click handler and by the periodic auto-refresh," but grep across `app/page.js` for `loadGmailMessages` returns exactly three references Ã¢â‚¬â€ its definition (`:903`), the initial call (`:925`), and the `setInterval` call (`:926`) Ã¢â‚¬â€ no click handler calls it any more. **Fix**: correct the interval constant to match the intended cadence (an owner call on whether 30 minutes or 60 seconds is actually wanted, not assumed here), stop the effect from firing an extra immediate call on every `applicationData.length` change, and delete the stale click-handler sentence at `:901-902`. | The one-line interval-constant fix (`app/page.js:926`) is safe to land independently Ã¢â‚¬â€ it does not touch the swallowed-refusal handling (backlog N79) or the 403-cause work (backlog N80). The re-fire-on-count-change fix and the stale comment removal belong in the same small round since they sit in the same effect block | app/page.js:920-922 Ã¢â‚¬â€ the effect's own comment, "every 30 minutes"; app/page.js:926 Ã¢â‚¬â€ `setInterval(loadGmailMessages, 60 * 1000)`, actually every 60 seconds; read directly 2026-09-28, unmoved from the citation handed in; app/page.js:923-929 Ã¢â‚¬â€ the effect body: `:924` guard, `:925` unconditional initial call, `:926` setInterval, `:929` dependency array `[currentUser, applicationData.length]`; app/api/gmail/messages/route.js:1-4 Ã¢â‚¬â€ the full import list; no rate-limiter import present; app/page.js:901-902 Ã¢â‚¬â€ "Used both by the icon click handler and by the periodic auto-refresh"; grep for `loadGmailMessages` across app/page.js returns exactly three references (:903 def, :925, :926), no click handler found; the owner's downloaded activity log: two POST /api/gmail/messages 403s, 45 seconds apart, while every other request succeeded Ã¢â‚¬â€ consistent with an application-count change firing an extra call outside the interval's own cadence |

| N47 | **REGENERATING A PREP PACK DESTROYS IT BEFORE THE MODEL IS CALLED Ã¢â‚¬â€ if generation then fails, the candidate loses the pack they had and gets nothing back. Live user-visible data loss, shipped by N29Ã¢â‚¬â„¢s Regenerate control.** **Verified by reading the applied function body**: `claim_prep_pack_slot` sets **`pack = '{}'::jsonb` UNCONDITIONALLY** on the `on conflict do update` branch (`20260922000000_interview_prep_remove_spend_caps.sql:86-97`, carried forward unchanged from the original `20260914000000`). The claim happens at the START of a generation attempt, so the existing pack is wiped the instant the candidate clicks Regenerate Ã¢â‚¬â€ before the prompt is built, before any provider call, before anything can succeed. Every later failure mode (model error, network, a refusal that empties the document, a kill-switch trip, the route timing out) therefore leaves the candidate with LESS than they started with. **The design is internally consistent, which is why this was not obvious**: `interview_prep_packs_running_has_no_content` (`20260914000000:179-180`) actively FORBIDS a `running` row from holding content, so the wipe is not an oversight Ã¢â‚¬â€ the schema requires it. That constraint is what makes this unfixable by a small patch and is the reason N45/N46Ã¢â‚¬â„¢s design moves the source of truth into an append-only revisions table: with revisions, the wipe becomes harmless because the previous content still exists elsewhere. **WORSE THAN FIRST RECORDED Ã¢â‚¬â€ found by the N45/N46 TDD seat, verified by me 2026-09-20: the loss does NOT require a failure.** GATE 10 (`route.js:384`, "Nothing to research: a deliberate, terminal row with NO model call") calls `finishAttempt` at `:386` and returns at `:393-394` Ã¢â‚¬â€ all of it AFTER `claim_prep_pack_slot` has already set `pack = '{}'`, and all of it BEFORE `readTrustedNames` at `:507`. So a regenerate on an application with no postable content destroys the pack on a deliberate, SUCCESSFUL terminal path, with no provider call and nothing that looks like an error. The original entry framed this as "if generation then fails"; that understated it. The UI's client-side description check (kept deliberately in N29) prevents the common case, but it is a client predicate over state that can go stale between load and click, and the route is reachable regardless. **This also makes the N47 test cheaper to write**: the path needs no model, no network and no failure injection. **SEVERITY, stated honestly**: the pack is regenerable, so this is lost WORK and lost TIME, not lost user-authored data Ã¢â‚¬â€ nothing the candidate typed is destroyed (their name and interviewer names live in separate tables). But a candidate regenerating the night before an interview, on a flaky connection, can be left with an empty pack and no way back. **THE UI DISCLOSURE IS DONE** (owner-chosen mitigation, shipped `08daed3`): the live caption at `PrepPackPanel.js:488-489` now reads "Regenerating clears the pack above the moment you start Ã¢â‚¬â€ before the new one is ready. If this attempt fails, thereÃ¢â‚¬â„¢s nothing to fall back to." **CORRECTION 2026-09-20**: this entry previously quoted the OLD `:215` string as though it were current. It was not Ã¢â‚¬â€ the copy fix had landed hours earlier. The stale quote propagated into the N45 designÃ¢â‚¬â„¢s Ã‚Â§9.4 too, and was caught by the N45/N46 AC round. The RISK is unchanged and still live; only the disclosure half is closed. **THREE WAYS OUT, and the owner should pick**: (a) fix it properly via N45/N46Ã¢â‚¬â„¢s revisions table, which makes the wipe safe Ã¢â‚¬â€ the real fix, but a whole chunk; (b) correct the warning copy now so the risk is at least disclosed Ã¢â‚¬â€ cheap, honest, does not reduce the risk; (c) gate Regenerate behind a confirmation until (a) lands Ã¢â‚¬â€ note this reverses the standing AC-N29.13 no-confirmation ruling, which was made when the action was believed cheap and repeatable. It is repeatable; it is not safe. **Do NOT "fix" this by making `claim_prep_pack_slot` preserve `pack`** Ã¢â‚¬â€ the CHECK constraint forbids it, and `interviewPrepEffectiveSchema.test.js:545-551` pins that function against modification | An OWNER pick between (a) N45/N46Ã¢â‚¬â„¢s revisions table, (b) honest warning copy now, and (c) a temporary confirmation gate Ã¢â‚¬â€ (b) is cheap and can ship immediately regardless of the others | `supabase/migrations/20260922000000_interview_prep_remove_spend_caps.sql:86-97` Ã¢â‚¬â€ `pack = '{}'::jsonb` set unconditionally on the claimÃ¢â‚¬â„¢s do-update branch, read directly 2026-09-20; `supabase/migrations/20260914000000_interview_prep.sql:179-180` Ã¢â‚¬â€ `interview_prep_packs_running_has_no_content` FORBIDS a running row holding content, so the wipe is required by the schema, not accidental; `app/components/tracking/PrepPackPanel.js:488-489` Ã¢â‚¬â€ the CURRENT caption, shipped `08daed3`, which already discloses the start-of-attempt clearing; the older `:215` quote this entry carried was stale; `lib/interviewPrep/interviewPrepEffectiveSchema.test.js:545-551` Ã¢â‚¬â€ pins `claim_prep_pack_slot` against modification; `<scratchpad>/chunks/N45/design-structure.r1.md` Ã¢â‚¬â€ found this while designing per-section revisions; its append-only table is what makes the wipe harmless; N29 shipped the Regenerate control in `0fa1103`; AC-N29.13 ruled no confirmation on the premise that the action is cheap and repeatable |

| N49 | **OWNER REQUEST 2026-09-20, TOP PRIORITY: thorough research on interview STAGES and the QUESTIONS asked in each Ã¢â‚¬â€ split into TWO DISTINCT CATEGORIES, VERIFIED and POSSIBLE.** The owner wants to know, for a given company and role, which stages are actually documented and which are inferred, and the same split for the questions within each stage. **WHY THIS IS NOT A PROMPT TWEAK: the schema cannot express it today.** A `Stage` is `{name, questions[], recommendedAnswer, support}` with **ONE `support` covering all three** (`route.js:246`, `normalizeStage`) Ã¢â‚¬â€ so there is no way to say "this stage is verified from a source, but these particular questions are an inference." N43/N48 hit this exact limit and had to attach the stage citation marker once, to `stage.name`, with a caption warning readers not to over-read it as per-question precision. **This item is the principled fix for that workaround.** **THE TWO CATEGORIES, and the distinction must survive into the DATA, not just the wording**: **VERIFIED** = attested by a citable source (the companyÃ¢â‚¬â„¢s own careers or engineering pages, a job posting that names its process, a dated first-hand candidate report) Ã¢â‚¬â€ must carry a real publisher URL, subject to the existing K1-SHAPE citation contract and to `safeExternalHref`. **POSSIBLE** = an inference from role, level, industry or company size, with NO source Ã¢â‚¬â€ legitimate and genuinely useful, but it must never be presentable as verified. **The failure mode to design against is one-directional**: a POSSIBLE item mislabelled VERIFIED is the harmful error (a candidate prepares for a stage that does not exist, or walks in citing a process the company does not run); a VERIFIED item shown as POSSIBLE merely under-sells. **Fail toward POSSIBLE**, exactly as N43Ã¢â‚¬â„¢s unverifiable-URL state fails toward inert. **WHAT MUST BE SETTLED**: (a) the schema Ã¢â‚¬â€ per-stage AND per-question provenance, which is a `support` shape change, so it interacts with N45/N46Ã¢â‚¬â„¢s section-owned claims (a stageÃ¢â‚¬â„¢s claims are already being moved into its own revision row) and must be designed with them or it reopens both; (b) where the research comes from Ã¢â‚¬â€ the company-research/digest pipeline already exists (`app/api/company-research/route.js`, `lib/tracking/digestCitations.js`) and should be reused, not rebuilt; (c) what the candidate SEES Ã¢â‚¬â€ two visually distinct treatments, where POSSIBLE is unmistakably not a claim about this employer; (d) whether a stage with zero verified evidence still appears at all. **DO NOT confuse this with K1-PROHIBITION**, which forbids predicting which specific PERSON will conduct an interview. Predicting the SHAPE of a process and the LIKELY questions is the featureÃ¢â‚¬â„¢s entire point and is not forbidden Ã¢â‚¬â€ but it must be labelled as prediction. **WHERE THE EVIDENCE ACTUALLY LIVES Ã¢â‚¬â€ OWNER CORRECTION 2026-09-20, and it reshapes the whole research design.** I had assumed careers pages and job postings. The owner is right that companies largely do NOT publish this; **candidate reports do** Ã¢â‚¬â€ Glassdoor interview reviews, Blind, Levels.fyi, Reddit threads, LinkedIn posts. That changes three things. **(1) What "VERIFIED" can mean.** A candidate report is an ATTESTATION, not an authoritative publication: it is one personÃ¢â‚¬â„¢s experience, of one team, at one level, on one date. So a row-level control cannot label itself Generate vs Regenerate, cannot know the pack is already `running`, and cannot honestly disable itself. **THAT IS THE REAL WORK IN THIS ITEM, and it is bigger than moving a button**: either (a) add a per-row prep-state prefetch alongside the existing digest prefetch, so the row can label and disable honestly Ã¢â‚¬â€ costs a query on the tracking list, and the design must say what it fetches and how often; or (b) ship a state-less row control with honest generic copy that resolves state only after activation Ã¢â‚¬â€ cheaper, but it must never claim something it cannot know, and it must not double-fire against an already-running generation. **Do not pick (b) by default because it is easier**; the whole point of the ownerÃ¢â‚¬â„¢s request is fewer clicks, and a control that fires and then reports "already running" spends a click to learn something a prefetch would have shown. **Also in scope**: the tracking row already carries Prep / JD / Resume / Comms, so a fifth control needs a real answer on crowding, especially at phone width (`mobileSx.js`). **Keep the in-panel control too, or remove it** Ã¢â‚¬â€ state which, because two controls doing the same thing in two places is its own defect. **Sequencing**: N29Ã¢â‚¬â„¢s adversarial verification is in flight over these exact files (`PrepPackPanel.js`, `AppViewDialog.js`, `ApplicationCard.js`, `TrackingTab.js`); this must not start until that lands, or the two will collide in one working tree. **ADOPTED DEFAULTS (reversible), AC round 2026-09-21** (`<scratchpad>/chunks/N42/ac.r1.md` Ã‚Â§7): (1) no confirmation before a row Regenerate Ã¢â‚¬â€ reversal: "confirm on Regenerate," costing one extra click on every regenerate. (2) the in-panel Generate/Regenerate control is kept alongside the new row control, sharing one queue and state, rather than being removed Ã¢â‚¬â€ reversal: "remove it," which deletes `GenerateControl` from the panel and re-keys N50's names-first hierarchy ruling. (3) the row control fires immediately without first asking for interviewer names Ã¢â‚¬â€ reversal: require names first, as the in-panel path (N50 Ruling 2) already does; consequence of the default: a pack generated before names are entered can still lose lines that mention an un-named interviewer | A design round deciding prefetch-vs-stateless (that is the actual decision, not the button placement), then the wave Ã¢â‚¬â€ starting only after N29Ã¢â‚¬â„¢s verification has landed | `<scratchpad>/chunks/N29/design-experience.r1.md` Ã¢â‚¬â€ chose Option B (in-panel) on state-awareness grounds; `ac.r1.md` Ã‚Â§6 left placement open with four deciding criteria; `app/components/AppViewDialog.js:111-125` Ã¢â‚¬â€ the pack is fetched only on dialog open; there is no per-row prep prefetch; `app/components/tracking/ApplicationCard.js` Ã¢â‚¬â€ the row already carries Prep / JD / Resume / Comms; a fifth control is a crowding question, especially at phone width; the digest precedent that DOES have a per-row prefetch (`digestsById`) Ã¢â‚¬â€ the model for option (a); N29 landed the in-panel control in `0fa1103`; this item moves or duplicates it |
| N41 | **OWNER DECISION 2026-09-20: remove BOTH interview-prep spend caps entirely.** The owner hit the retry limit and ruled that neither cap stays. **What exists today, verified by reading the migration**: `interview_prep_spend` is keyed `application_id uuid primary key` (`20260914000000_interview_prep.sql:290-291`), so both counters are **per application**, not per account Ã¢â‚¬â€ a candidate with 100 tracked applications has 100 independent budgets. `attempts` is capped at 6 by `constraint interview_prep_spend_attempts_check check (attempts >= 0 and attempts <= 6)` (`:311-312`); `model_calls` is capped at 12 (`PREP_MODEL_CALLS_MAX`) and is deliberately NOT a CHECK, because a CHECK forbidding `model_calls > 12` would reject the very write recording the 12th call (`:298-306`). Both are refused together inside `claim_prep_pack_slot` at `:496`: `if v_attempts >= 6 or v_model_calls >= 12 then`. **THE COST EXPOSURE WAS PUT TO THE OWNER AND THEY CHOSE REMOVAL WITH IT STATED** Ã¢â‚¬â€ recorded here so it is not rediscovered as a surprise: `model_calls` is the PAID provider counter, and once it is gone, **provider spend is bounded only by the routeÃ¢â‚¬â„¢s rate limiting and the kill switch**. Those two therefore become load-bearing cost controls rather than convenience guards, and backlog records rate limiting as PARTIAL across the repo. A stuck retry loop or a runaway client now has no per-application ceiling. This is the ownerÃ¢â‚¬â„¢s call and is not to be re-litigated; it is recorded because the next person to touch spend needs to know the ceiling was removed deliberately. **WHAT THE WORK ACTUALLY IS Ã¢â‚¬â€ not a flag flip**: both caps live in the DATABASE. (a) Drop or widen the `attempts` CHECK constraint; (b) remove the `v_attempts >= 6 or v_model_calls >= 12` guard from `claim_prep_pack_slot`Ã¢â‚¬â„¢s body, which is a `create or replace function` in a migration. **This needs a NEW migration with a later timestamp Ã¢â‚¬â€ NEVER an edit to `20260914000000` in place**; N30 records that exact hazard already firing in this repo, where an edited-in-place migration was silently skipped by `db push` because it keys on the version stamp, leaving the committed text describing a database that does not exist. (c) Application-side: `attemptsExhausted` becomes permanently false, so the "attempts-exhausted" state disappears from the prep surface Ã¢â‚¬â€ note N29 records that this prop is currently passed to `PrepPackPanel` and silently dropped anyway. (d) Any test asserting the 6/12 bounds must be updated, not deleted Ã¢â‚¬â€ and the replacement must still prove `claim_prep_pack_slot` increments correctly, since the counters remain even without ceilings. **KEEP, despite the caps going**: the client-side check that a posting actually has a description before firing. That no longer rests on a scarce counter but on not spending money and the userÃ¢â‚¬â„¢s time on a call that cannot produce anything | A migration round Ã¢â‚¬â€ new timestamped migration only Ã¢â‚¬â€ plus the application-side removal of the exhausted state and updates to every test asserting the 6/12 bounds. Sequenced WITH N29, whose design assumed scarcity | `supabase/migrations/20260914000000_interview_prep.sql:290-291` Ã¢â‚¬â€ `interview_prep_spend` keyed by `application_id`, so both caps are per application; `:311-312` Ã¢â‚¬â€ `check (attempts >= 0 and attempts <= 6)`; `:298-306` Ã¢â‚¬â€ why `model_calls` has no CHECK; `:496` Ã¢â‚¬â€ `if v_attempts >= 6 or v_model_calls >= 12 then`, the single guard enforcing both; backlog N30 Ã¢â‚¬â€ the edit-an-applied-migration hazard, already fired once in this repo; backlog N29 Ã¢â‚¬â€ its design round assumed attempt scarcity and was corrected mid-flight by this ruling; rate limiting is recorded as PARTIAL repo-wide, and it plus the kill switch are now the only spend guards |

| N35 | **OWNER REQUEST 2026-09-20, second-highest priority. Feed the company-facts pipeline into the cover letter and the outreach email Ã¢â‚¬â€ but ONLY facts the candidate has verified through the preview/edit modal.** The app already researches a company and already writes cover letters, and the two never meet: the tailor path (`app/api/tailor/route.js`) generates the cover letter, while company facts live in a separate pipeline (`app/api/company-research/route.js`, `lib/tracking/digestCitations.js`, `app/copilot/CompanyBriefPanel.js`, `app/components/CompanyResearchDialog.js`). So a cover letter today says nothing specific about the employer, which is the single thing that makes a cover letter read as written-for-them. **THE GATE IS THE POINT, and it is what makes this item different from "put the digest in the prompt"**: a company fact is exactly the kind of claim a model will confidently invent, and an invented fact about the employer, in a document the candidate SENDS, is the worst failure this app can produce Ã¢â‚¬â€ strictly worse than a thin letter. So no fact may reach a letter or an email until the candidate has seen it and accepted it in the preview/edit modal. **Design constraints, carried from what this repo already learned**: (i) the pack/digest work already established that grounding claims need real publisher URLs Ã¢â‚¬â€ `web.uri` values from Gemini grounding are vertexaisearch REDIRECTS, so any host-based corroboration check silently drops every citation (see the grounding memory and N26-era rulings); a verified fact must carry a real source the candidate can click. (ii) The verification state must be STORED and must be what the generator reads Ã¢â‚¬â€ not re-derived, and not a flag the model can write. This is structurally the same trust problem as N33Ã¢â‚¬â„¢s user-supplied names: the only values that get privileged treatment are the ones the USER accepted. Reuse that ruling rather than inventing a second mechanism. (iii) Standing directive "minimize clicks": verification is the one confirmation that earns its keep here, so it must be ONE pass over the facts with good defaults Ã¢â‚¬â€ not a per-fact wizard. (iv) Say explicitly what happens to a letter generated when ZERO facts are verified; that is the common case on day one and it must degrade to todayÃ¢â‚¬â„¢s behaviour, not to a broken document. **UNMEASURED, and a scoping round must measure it first**: nothing here has been measured Ã¢â‚¬â€ not how many facts a typical digest yields, not how many survive verification, not whether the email path exists as a generator at all (`lib/gmail/gmailClient.js` sends; whether anything GENERATES the outreach email body is unestablished). Do not let an implementer infer these. **RE-PLAN GATE FINDING 2026-09-23: the plan's Step 0 gate is now VOID, because the chunk it gates on was abandoned, not delayed.** `plan.r2.md`'s G0.1 ("the rebuild line-break fix landed") requires `git show origin/main:hello-world/lib/document/docx.js` to contain a line matching `function paragraphSegments(` AND the three rebuild test files to pass in a clean `git archive origin/main` extract, before Step 0 Ã¢â‚¬â€ and therefore Step 1's migration push Ã¢â‚¬â€ can proceed. That helper and those tests do not exist on `origin/main` and never will under that plan: `3ba214c` (2026-09-23) reverted the entire unshipped rebuild after eight NOT-SHIP verification rounds (see backlog N54) and deleted the four test files G0.1(b) names. The gate was written to read FAIL today and PASS once that chunk landed; it will now read FAIL FOREVER, because there is nothing left to land Ã¢â‚¬â€ a gate that can never pass is not a landing precondition, it is a silent permanent block. **Why the gate was there, and why it still matters even though the mechanism it names is gone**: accepting a fact writes new bytes into `coverLetterDocxB64` (design r2 Ã‚Â§2.4/Ã‚Â§2.10 step 5, `factDocx.js`'s `applyCoverDocxEdits`/`spliceParagraph`, a direct in-place paragraph splice that explicitly REFUSES any paragraph containing a `w:br`/`w:cr`/`w:tab` with `"unsupported"`, so it cannot itself corrupt a break-bearing paragraph like the sign-off). But `acceptFacts` never marks the cover letter `edited` (design r2 Ã‚Â§2.10 step 9: "edited is never assigned"), so the letter stays on the ordinary rebuild path for the candidate's OWN next hand edit Ã¢â‚¬â€ and that rebuild is `resolveDocumentBlob`/`buildDocxFromUploadedTemplate`, the exact mechanism the eight rounds could not land (backlog N54). A candidate who accepts a fact and then makes any hand edit anywhere in the letter Ã¢â‚¬â€ not necessarily to the sign-off Ã¢â‚¬â€ still hits the live defect: `"Sincerely,[br]Alex Shaw"` rebuilds as `"Since[br]rely,"` with a stray paragraph. This was already true for any hand-edited embedded cover letter before N35 existed; N35 makes it common rather than incidental, because accepting a fact is the one-click action this chunk exists to ship, and a candidate who accepts a company fact is exactly the candidate likely to proofread and hand-edit the resulting letter afterward. **This chunk needs a re-plan before it is built, not a resumed Step 0**: either (a) it ships with this limit stated plainly in the AC and this backlog entry Ã¢â‚¬â€ accepting a fact can still be followed by a hand edit that drops the sign-off's break, exactly as it can today for any embedded cover letter Ã¢â‚¬â€ or (b) Step 0 is rewritten to wait on backlog N54 instead of on a specific named helper that no longer has anywhere to land. Do not silently drop G0.1 and proceed; that would ship past the exact risk the original plan flagged as blocking. **ADOPTED DEFAULTS (reversible), AC round 2026-09-21** (`<scratchpad>/chunks/N40/ac.r1.md` Ã‚Â§7 Ã¢â‚¬â€ filed under N40's chunk path by a labelling slip the file itself flags; the content is N35's): (1) the fact source is research-modal cards only, not digest prose or copilot Ã¢â‚¬â€ reversal: "also let me pick sentences from the digest." | N56 | **Accepting two or more research cards in ONE click always fails, and the candidate is shown the WRONG reason Ã¢â‚¬â€ live, user-facing defect on the shipped N35 accept path.** **Root cause, verified by reading the code.** `app/components/CompanyResearchDialog.js`Ã¢â‚¬â„¢s one-click "Insert into cover letter" button, on the `pick` step, calls `onAccept(acceptSelection())` directly (`:365-372`) Ã¢â‚¬â€ it never visits the `arrange` step, which is the ONLY place a per-card placement `Select` renders (`:233-237`). Every fact `acceptSelection()` builds therefore carries `placement: c.target` (`:131`), and `c.target` is `targets[a.id] || DEFAULT_PLACEMENT` (`:117`) with every `targets[a.id]` initialized to `DEFAULT_PLACEMENT` (`:90`) and never changed on this path. So two selected cards always plan against the SAME paragraph. `lib/acceptedFacts/factInsertion.js`Ã¢â‚¬â„¢s `planCoverFacts` (`:61-88`) builds its edits against one mutable `out` array, applying each fact in turn, so the SECOND editÃ¢â‚¬â„¢s `before` is captured AFTER the first edit already wrote `out[index] = after` for that index Ã¢â‚¬â€ `edits[1].before === edits[0].after`. `lib/acceptedFacts/factDocx.js`Ã¢â‚¬â„¢s `applyCoverDocxEdits` then checks each editÃ¢â‚¬â„¢s `before` against `originalLines`, the UN-modified lines the plan was built from (`:33-34`: `if (lines[edit.lineIndex] !== edit.before) return { docxB64, applied: false, reason: "stale-plan" }`), and for the second edit that check always fails, because `originalLines[index]` is still the pre-accept text while `edit.before` is the first editÃ¢â‚¬â„¢s output. **The candidate is then shown the WRONG message.** `app/hooks/useCompanyResearch.js:308-310` maps EVERY `!spliced.applied` outcome, `stale-plan` included, onto `NO_ENGINE_BYTES_REASON` (`:18-20`): "We can't add this to your cover letter right now because its saved file is missing. Regenerate the cover letter in this session, then try again." The file is not missing; nothing tells the candidate to accept one card at a time; and "regenerate the cover letter" actively discards the one thing that would fix it. **Reachable on an ordinary path**: the dialog pre-selects every fetched article by default (`:86`, `setSelected(new Set(...))`), so researching a company and clicking the single visible "Insert into cover letter" button reproduces this whenever two or more articles were found. **This is why `app/hooks/acceptFactIdCollision.test.js` deliberately accepts exactly ONE card per click in every scenario** Ã¢â‚¬â€ `runSessionAndAccept(RUN_1)` and `runSessionAndAccept(RUN_2)` are always two SEPARATE sessions, never one call with both, and its own "one session, one click, one write carrying that run's fact" test asserts `body.facts` has length 1; a same-click multi-card accept in that harness would abort before the PUT is ever sent, which would mask the very id-collision assertions that file exists to make. **Fix direction**: apply `planCoverFacts`Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ edits in descending `lineIndex` order so a later editÃ¢â‚¬â„¢s target paragraph is still untouched when it is planned, or re-plan against the progressively-updated `out` document so `factDocx.js` checks against the SAME lines the plan actually used rather than the original snapshot; and separately, `useCompanyResearch.js:308-310` must stop collapsing every `applied:false` reason onto the missing-file copy Ã¢â‚¬â€ a `stale-plan` refusal needs its own, accurate message. | A fix round on the shipped N35 accept path Ã¢â‚¬â€ the edit-ordering or re-planning fix in `factInsertion.js`/`factDocx.js`, plus a corrected, reason-specific refusal message in `useCompanyResearch.js`. A red test that accepts 2+ cards in one click and asserts BOTH facts land in the letter is the regression guard to add first | `app/components/CompanyResearchDialog.js:365-372` (one-click accept never visits `arrange`), `:233-237` (the placement `Select`, arrange-step only), `:90`/`:117`/`:131` (every card defaults to and keeps `DEFAULT_PLACEMENT` on the accept path), `:86` (every fetched article pre-selected); `lib/acceptedFacts/factInsertion.js:61-88` (`planCoverFacts` mutates one shared `out` array across facts, so a later editÃ¢â‚¬â„¢s `before` is an earlier editÃ¢â‚¬â„¢s `after` when both target the same index); `lib/acceptedFacts/factDocx.js:33-34` (`applied:false, reason:"stale-plan"` Ã¢â‚¬â€ the check that fires for the second edit); `app/hooks/useCompanyResearch.js:18-20` (`NO_ENGINE_BYTES_REASON` text) and `:308-310` (every `!spliced.applied` reason, `stale-plan` included, mapped to that same text); `app/hooks/acceptFactIdCollision.test.js` Ã¢â‚¬â€ every scenario calls `runSessionAndAccept` once per fact (`RUN_1`, then separately `RUN_2`), and its own harness-sanity test asserts a single-fact accept produces `body.facts` of length 1; read directly, not restated |
| N59 | **Accepted facts do not survive a reload Ã¢â‚¬â€ a durability gap in the shipped N35 accept path.** Fact TEXT persists as a new `generated_cover_letters` version (via the `accept_application_facts` RPC, migration `20260923030000_application_accepted_facts.sql`), but the SPLICED docx bytes produced by `applyCoverDocxEdits` live only in React state Ã¢â‚¬â€ session-only, confirmed by `app/hooks/useCompanyResearch.js`Ã¢â‚¬â„¢s own `SESSION_ONLY_NOTICE` comment (`:27-34`) disclosing exactly this limit at accept time. **Three points verified together show the gap is real, not just disclosed.** (1) `app/page.js:485-497` persists only a slim per-job summary to `localStorage` on every `tailoringMap` change Ã¢â‚¬â€ `status`, `error`, `downloaded`, `generatedJobTitle` Ã¢â‚¬â€ no `coverLetterResultLines`, no `coverLetterDocxB64`. (2) `app/hooks/useDocumentPreview.js:255`, inside `selectDocumentVersion` (`:227`), deliberately blanks `coverLetterDocxB64: ""` on a cover-letter version switch, with its own comment explaining why: no `docx_path` column exists on `generated_cover_letters` to substitute, so "right content unformatted beats wrong content formatted" is the accepted tradeoff. Whichever is chosen, **record the MEASURED runtime beside the constant** so the next person can tell drift from load. **Do NOT "fix" this by marking them flaky or retrying** Ã¢â‚¬â€ a retry hides a genuine slowdown, and these tests get slower as the repo grows, which is the real signal. **FIFTH FILE, 2026-09-20**: `lib/llm/grounding.test.js` joined the set during N43\u2019s TDD round \u2014 failed in a full run, passed alone at 31/31 immediately after (orchestrator-verified). It is NOT a whole-tree scanner like the other four, which widens the diagnosis: this is general CPU contention under a large parallel suite, not only tree-walk cost, so a shared cached scan fixes the original four but would NOT have caught this one. | N24 | **The live interview panel is too small to be useful on non-mobile screens.** Owner report, 2026-09-15. The copilot dashboard is the surface a candidate reads WHILE being interviewed, and on a laptop or desktop it wastes most of the window. **Grounded in the constraints that produce it, not a vibe**: the sticky question band is capped at **30% of the viewport height** - `BAND_BASE_DVH` in `app/copilot/stickyQuestionGuards.test.js:48` pins `max(calc(64px + 4.25rem), calc((100dvh - var(--sticky-top, 0px)) * 0.30))` - so on a 1080p screen the question and its answer share roughly a third of the height while two thirds sit empty. The voice-cue rail then takes a fixed **280px** at `md` and up (`app/copilot/VoiceCueSidebar.js:30` `RAIL_SX`), which is a far larger proportional bite at 1280px than the layout was tuned for. The column arithmetic (`CopilotClient.js:862`, `:873`, `:904`) is a `flex: 1 / minHeight: 0` chain written so the pieces fit on a phone, and it holds the same shape all the way up. **Almost every sizing decision in this surface was made for the phone case and never re-judged for the desktop one** - see [[copilot-mobile]], which records the mobile pass; there has been no desktop equivalent. **What this must NOT become**: more panels visible at once. The candidate is reading this while a person waits for them to speak, so the goal is BIGGER TYPE AND FEWER THINGS, not denser information - the [[minimize-clicks]] reasoning applied to attention rather than clicks. **A design round should settle**: what the desktop breakpoint gives back to the answer area; whether the rail collapses, docks or moves at `lg`+; whether the band cap should be a function of available height rather than a fixed fraction; and what the reading distance actually is (a laptop at arm length is not a phone at 30cm, and the type scale never changed). **Measurement limit, stated honestly**: this repo cannot measure real layout - jsdom computes no geometry and the browser pane needs a visible window ([[measurement-instruments]]). The design round needs real screenshots at real breakpoints, which is an owner-run instrument | A design round (1c) for the desktop breakpoint, then the wave that builds it | `app/copilot/stickyQuestionGuards.test.js:48` (`BAND_BASE_DVH` - the 30%-of-viewport cap); `app/copilot/VoiceCueSidebar.js:30` (`RAIL_SX` - fixed 280px at md+); `app/copilot/CopilotClient.js:862`, `:873`, `:904` (the flex/minHeight column chain); [[copilot-mobile]] records the mobile pass; no desktop equivalent exists |
| N17 | **Voice enrollment: identify the candidate's own voice from an uploaded sample of them reading an app-provided passage.** Owner request, 2026-09-14. **Scope is narrower than it sounds, and the survey is why**: for a REMOTE interview the app already separates the two voices by CHANNEL Ã¢â‚¬â€ `lib/copilot/capture.js` takes the candidate's own voice from `getUserMedia` (mic) and the other party's from `getDisplayMedia` (tab/system audio), so enrollment adds nothing there. The case that actually needs this is the **SHARED MICROPHONE / in-person** session, which is exactly what `lib/copilot/speakerIdentity.js` exists for today Ã¢â‚¬â€ and it identifies the candidate BEHAVIOURALLY (word share, question detection, confidence gates), not by voice. **The motivating failure is already documented in that file's header**: v1 scored on word share alone and elected the INTERVIEWER as the user at 'high' confidence on two real interview openings, after which the copilot stopped evaluating their speech for questions and went **silently deaf for the rest of the session** Ã¢â‚¬â€ the file calls this the worst failure mode the feature has, because the user cannot tell it happened. A voiceprint replaces a heuristic that is known to fail silently, which is the strongest case for building it. **THE FEASIBILITY CRUX, unresolved**: diarization as wired today (`lib/copilot/stt/index.js`, Deepgram and ElevenLabs Scribe) yields ANONYMOUS speaker tags (`speakerTag` = provider-assigned id), never an identity Ã¢â‚¬â€ binding tagÃ¢â€ â€™person needs either provider-side speaker enrollment/verification or a locally-run speaker-embedding model, and NEITHER is confirmed available on the current providers. That question must be settled at primary source BEFORE any UI is designed, or the enrollment flow gets built against a capability that does not exist. **Why an app-provided passage is the right call** (the owner asked for it and it is correct): a known transcript gives phonetic coverage, lets the app verify the upload is actually someone reading THAT passage rather than arbitrary audio, and supports a quality gate (too short, too noisy, clipped) before a bad enrollment silently degrades every later session. **A voiceprint is BIOMETRIC data** Ã¢â‚¬â€ distinct from the recordings the copilot already stores Ã¢â‚¬â€ **OWNER RULING, 2026-09-14: storing the candidate's voice is PERMITTED, provided they are made aware of it and consent.** That settles whether the feature may exist. The auto path received the id-level guard (`useCompanyResearch.js:694-699`, `insertedIds`); the manual path did not. **Demonstrated**, not inferred: two `planAcceptForEntry` runs on the same fact with placement intro -> current yield occ2 = 2, a visible duplicate. **Reachability**: `selected` resets empty on each dialog open (`app/components/CompanyResearchDialog.js:71`), so it needs a deliberate re-check of an already-accepted article; the card then seeds to the new default (`:95`) and accept re-inserts at the new line. Renumbered from N85 on 2026-09-28: another seat had already committed a different N85 (the arrange-step duplicate) in db7492d Ã¢â‚¬â€ this is a follow-up, not a regression in it. `app/hooks/useCoverFactPlacement.js:32` seeds `placement` at `""`. BOTH failure paths land on the same value: the `.catch(() => {})` at `:41-43`, and the non-ok GET at `:36-39` (`r.ok ? r.json() : null` -> data null -> `settable(undefined)` false -> no update). The author's comment at `:29-31` shows they reasoned about "a fresh user never pinned anything" but not about "the load failed or is still in flight." **THE SHARPER HALF**: `app/components/CoverFactPlacementSetting.js` and `app/page.js:245` each call this hook SEPARATELY, each running its own fetch with NO shared cache. | N89 | **OWNER-BLOCKING, 2026-09-28: FACT INSERTION REFUSES ON EVERY GEMINI COVER LETTER because the Gemini engine never produces a server-side document.** The owner's activity log (activity-log-2026-09-28-2105.md) shows `fact-auto-insert.failed code:no-engine-bytes` on a FRESH in-session tailoring (#76 /api/tailor 200 in 53s Ã¢â€ â€™ #90 company-research Ã¢â€ â€™ #91 failure), so this is NOT the reloaded-letter case N59 addressed and is NOT deploy lag (N59 fix 33ee886 landed 19:50Z, log is 21:05Z). ROOT CAUSE: `lib/llm/engines/geminiEngine.js:16-17` states it Ã¢â‚¬â€ Gemini fills the user's UPLOADED TEMPLATE client-side and never returns a finished document (no docxB64); `tailorCoverLetter` returns `{engine, result, resultLines}` only. `app/hooks/useCompanyResearch.js`'s `resolveCoverEngineBytes` (:212) needs either in-session `entry.coverLetterDocxB64` or a resolvable `coverLetterDocxPath`; for Gemini BOTH are always empty, so auto-insert (:724) and manual accept (:428, :619) all hit `no-engine-bytes` (:732). The whole N56/N61 insertion mechanism assumes engine-produced docx bytes to splice into; the Gemini engine by design has none. **The download path already solves the same problem the right way**: `lib/document/docx.js:754` resolves `coverLetterDocxB64 || coverLetterTemplateDocxB64 || ""` and `resolveDocumentBlob` rebuilds from `uploadedTemplate: coverLetterFile` (the in-session uploaded template File) + `coverLetterResultLines` Ã¢â‚¬â€ so an EDITED Gemini cover letter already downloads correctly by re-filling the template from lines. **FIX DIRECTION**: fact insertion for the no-engine-bytes case must operate on `coverLetterResultLines` (insert the fact into the lines at the resolved placement, apply the same id+presence dedupe as N81) and let the existing template-rebuild produce the document, instead of requiring docx bytes to splice. **HARD EDGE to design, do not skip**: for a RELOADED Gemini letter the uploaded `coverLetterFile` is gone (a File object, never persisted) and only `content_lines` survive Ã¢â‚¬â€ so a line-level insert can happen but the ORIGINAL template formatting cannot be reproduced; decide honestly between (a) inserting into lines and rebuilding onto a plain document with a clear notice that original template formatting needs a regenerate, or (b) refusing a reloaded Gemini letter with an honest message distinct from today's misleading one. Also reconcile with N54 (the setParagraphText corruption seam that edited-line rebuilds route through) and the memory note download-rebuild-formatting. **STOPGAP the owner can use immediately**: the embedded engine DOES produce a server-side docx (tailor-lite renders from a template buffer, returns docxB64), so facts insert into embedded-engine cover letters today; Gemini was the specific broken case. | AC Ã¢â€ â€™ design Ã¢â€ â€™ plan Ã¢â€ â€™ TDD Ã¢â€ â€™ implement Ã¢â€ â€™ verify, top priority | lib/llm/engines/geminiEngine.js:16-17,26-28 Ã¢â‚¬â€ Gemini fills the uploaded template client-side, returns no docxB64 for the cover letter; app/hooks/useCompanyResearch.js:212-220 resolveCoverEngineBytes Ã¢â‚¬â€ needs coverLetterDocxB64 or coverLetterDocxPath; both empty for Gemini; app/hooks/useCompanyResearch.js:732 Ã¢â‚¬â€ no-engine-bytes refusal; lib/document/docx.js:754 and resolveDocumentBlob(uploadedTemplate) Ã¢â‚¬â€ the download path already rebuilds a Gemini cover letter from template + lines, the pattern to reuse; activity-log-2026-09-28-2105.md #76/#90/#91 Ã¢â‚¬â€ fresh tailoring, then no-engine-bytes; 53s tailor time indicates the Gemini (LLM) engine, not the instant embedded engine; lib/llm/engines/tailor-lite/engine.js tailorCoverLetter returns docxB64 Ã¢â‚¬â€ the embedded engine works today, the stopgap |
| N90 | **OWNER-BLOCKING follow-up to N89, 2026-09-28: an explicit ACCEPT never un-declines the fact it is accepting, so a removed-then-re-accepted fact is suppressed forever and every later auto-insert returns `nothing-eligible`.** Root cause CONFIRMED by the N89 diagnostic seat (docs/loop/N89.diagnosis.r1.md), no re-investigation owed. Nothing ever clears an entry from the removed log. `removeInsertedFact` (app/hooks/useCompanyResearch.js:677-678, `removedKey = record.url || record.id`) is the only writer; `acceptFacts` (:445, `declinedUrls = acceptedFactsByJob[jobId]?.removed || []`) resends it UNCHANGED at :528 regardless of what is being accepted, and `autoInsertFactsForJob` (:910) resends it unchanged too (correctly Ã¢â‚¬â€ its eligibility filter :845-850 already excludes the removed set, so its inserts and its echoed log are disjoint by construction). So: user removes a fact (url enters removed log) Ã¢â€ â€™ reopens the research dialog (CompanyResearchDialog never filters removed cards, so the card is still selectable Ã¢â‚¬â€ reachable from shipped UI) Ã¢â€ â€™ re-accepts it Ã¢â€ â€™ the persisted row now has the SAME url in BOTH `facts` and `removed` Ã¢â€ â€™ any later fresh research + auto-insert drops it via `removedSet.has(a.url)` Ã¢â€ â€™ `nothing-eligible`, permanently. Matching is url-keyed and deterministic (mintArticleId hashes the normalised url), so the suppression is permanent, not flaky. **FIX 1 (acceptFacts only, :445):** replace the pass-through with `priorRemoved.filter((key) => !acceptedKeys.has(key))`, where `acceptedKeys` is a Set of this click's accepted facts' `.url` and `.id`. Scope: un-decline ONLY the keys being explicitly accepted this click; leave every other removed entry (a fact the candidate did not touch) suppressed. Do NOT touch `autoInsertFactsForJob` (an unrequested auto-insert must never resurrect a removed fact) or `removeInsertedFact` (removal still adds to the log). No server/RPC change (the RPC already replaces `retracted` with whatever the client sends). **FIX 2 (logging enrichment):** the `nothing-eligible` return of `autoInsertFactsForJob` must carry plain counts Ã¢â‚¬â€ `articleCount`, `droppedNoUrl`, `droppedNoSuggestion`, `droppedRemoved`, `droppedRemovedAlsoAccepted` (the last = dropped-as-removed urls that ALSO appear in the accepted `facts`, which directly exposes the bug). Add those five names to the `fact-auto-insert` `fields` allowlist in lib/activityLog/activityChannels.js:230 (N77 privacy: counts only, never a url/title/text), and thread them through DocumentPreviewMount.js's `recordAutoInsertOutcome`, GATED on `code === "nothing-eligible"` so no other outcome's record shape changes. | AC Ã¢â€ â€™ design Ã¢â€ â€™ plan Ã¢â€ â€™ TDD (this file's tests land RED) Ã¢â€ â€™ implement Ã¢â€ â€™ verify | docs/loop/N89.diagnosis.r1.md Ã¢â‚¬â€ confirmed root cause and both specs (source of truth); app/hooks/useCompanyResearch.js:445,528 Ã¢â‚¬â€ acceptFacts resends the removed log unchanged, never subtracting the keys being accepted; app/hooks/useCompanyResearch.js:677-678 Ã¢â‚¬â€ removeInsertedFact is the only writer of the removed log (removedKey = record.url || record.id); app/hooks/useCompanyResearch.js:845-853 Ã¢â‚¬â€ the eligibility filter drops any article whose url/id is in removedSet; returns code nothing-eligible when eligible is empty; app/components/CompanyResearchDialog.js:117-140 Ã¢â‚¬â€ the card list is never filtered against the removed log, so a previously-removed article stays selectable (reachable from shipped UI); lib/activityLog/activityChannels.js:230 Ã¢â‚¬â€ fact-auto-insert fields allowlist, currently [reason, count, code]; a field not listed is dropped by recordDecision's whitelist (appActivityLog.js:182-201); app/components/DocumentPreviewMount.js:44-51,254-255 Ã¢â‚¬â€ recordAutoInsertOutcome forwards the function result to recordDecision on the articles-resolved path |

(2) CLIENT DOUBLE-MOUNT: IdealAnswerAids mounts in two render sites, so two HTTP POSTs fire for the same tailored key; the SERVER in-flight promise dedupe coalesces them to ONE model call IN-PROCESS, but on serverless the two requests can land on DIFFERENT instances -> two model calls. State the ms budget as a number + what shows when a prefetch isn't ready yet. LIVE mode (interviewer's question, not app-generated) can't pre-know the exact question -> design the live-mode strategy (per-posting pool / warmed generic-but-role-right example) explicitly. This is a latency+structure (1b/1h) problem.** the benchmark 'example project' beside copilot answers is the WRONG KIND of project most of the time — the user ALWAYS wants an example, but keeps getting a generic tech-company story (backend scaling: queue backpressure, p95 latency, uptime %, throughput) with the posting's domain slotted in, e.g. an SRE story 'in Education'. (B) normalizeIdealProject (lib/copilot/idealProjectPrompt.js) REJECTS the whole model example if ANY digit run in it occurs anywhere in the posting (digitRuns/postingNumbers blunt whole-number match) — small invented integers (2,3,4,5, a percent) collide with incidental posting numbers constantly, so most generated examples are rejected -> deterministic fallback. PRIMARY = make the model-written, posting-grounded example reliably reach the user on the real path (A+B); the archetype path (C) is the still-imperfect fallback. ## Standing hazards — re-read before trusting a number

- **An instrument here is defective until a constructed mutant kills it.** The author's own claim that a mutant
  dies is not evidence: one wave reported a kill in good faith and the real mutant survived, because the mutant
  tested was easier than the named one (ledger `T3-S9-3`).
- **A count beside a list it does not match** has occurred three times — a table heading, a column enumeration,
  and a ledger figure asserted as 4 where `grep -c "module:"` returns **5**.
- **A claim restated from another document is not verified.** Six false claims propagated here purely by
  restatement, each one grep-checkable. Run the grep with a canary before repeating a fact.
- **Ruling on a check's blockers alone silently drops its majors.** Nine majors once sat unrouted through three
  revisions because a ruling answered only the blockers, and everything downstream read the check as handled.
